But I certainly don't feel like I can just go without sleep, and I wouldn't say I'm irritable... mostly just tired of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad or anything like that. I still like my usual hobbies and such. It's just... well, like I said, the hard part is sitting still long enough to actually do them. But no, my mood is fine. I haven't been feeling down or losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling excessively worried or anxious about a number of things, like work, health, or other everyday matters?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. I mean, I have normal everyday concerns, but I'm not excessively worried or anxious about my health or anything else. My mind isn't spinning with worries... it's really just that physical feeling of needing to be doing something, like I can't just sit and relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a discomfort or restlessness in your legs when you try to sit still or lie down, like a crawling or tingling sensation that makes you want to move them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get anything like that. It's like... well, I just have to jump up and do the next thing, even when I'm completely worn out and I know I should just be sitting still. It's just a constant push to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Is this inner restlessness something you feel all the time, even when you're not in situations where you need to be still, or does it mostly come up when you're trying to sit down or relax?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's pretty much all the time. I always have this urge to jump up and do the next thing, even when I'm completely worn out and really just need to be sitting still. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your mood being unusually elevated, irritable, or feeling like you don’t need as much sleep lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My mood isn't elevated or unusually high. And I definitely need my sleep. Like I said, I'm completely worn out. It's just hard to actually sit still and get that rest, you know? But I certainly don't feel like I can just go without sleep, and I wouldn't say I'm irritable... mostly just tired of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad or anything like that. I still like my usual hobbies and such. It's just... well, like I said, the hard part is sitting still long enough to actually do them. But no, my mood is fine. I haven't been feeling down or losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling excessively worried or anxious about a number of things, like work, health, or other everyday matters?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. I mean, I have normal everyday concerns, but I'm not excessively worried or anxious about my health or anything else. My mind isn't spinning with worries... it's really just that physical feeling of needing to be doing something, like I can't just sit and relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a discomfort or restlessness in your legs when you try to sit still or lie down, like a crawling or tingling sensation that makes you want to move them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get anything like that. My legs don't tingle or crawl or feel uncomfortable like that. It's not a physical feeling in my legs specifically making me move them... it's just a general feeling of needing to be up and doing something. But no weird sensations in my legs.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what it feels like when you can't sit still? Is it more of an inner restlessness or a mental push to keep moving, even when you know you should be resting?\n\nPatient: It's definitely an inner restlessness. Honestly, that's really why I came in. I just feel like I cannot settle inside my own skin anymore. It's like... well, I just have to jump up and do the next thing, even when I'm completely worn out and I know I should just be sitting still. It's just a constant push to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Is this inner restlessness something you feel all the time, even when you're not in situations where you need to be still, or does it mostly come up when you're trying to sit down or relax?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's pretty much all the time. I mean... I guess I notice it the most when I actually try to sit down in my chair and relax, because that's when it really bothers me that I can't just be still. But even when I'm up and doing things around the house, I still feel that push. It's just always there, like my motor is always running, no matter what I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where your restlessness was so intense that it significantly interfered with your ability to function at work, socially, or in other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely interferes with things. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your mood being unusually elevated, irritable, or feeling like you don’t need as much sleep lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My mood isn't elevated or unusually high. And I definitely need my sleep. Like I said, I'm completely worn out. It's just hard to actually sit still and get that rest, you know? But I certainly don't feel like I can just go without sleep, and I wouldn't say I'm irritable... mostly just tired of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad or anything like that. I still like my usual hobbies and such. It's just... well, like I said, the hard part is sitting still long enough to actually do them. But no, my mood is fine. I haven't been feeling down or losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling excessively worried or anxious about a number of things, like work, health, or other everyday matters?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. I mean, I have normal everyday concerns, but I'm not excessively worried or anxious about my health or anything else. My mind isn't spinning with worries... it's really just that physical feeling of needing to be doing something, like I can't just sit and relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a discomfort or restlessness in your legs when you try to sit still or lie down, like a crawling or tingling sensation that makes you want to move them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get anything like that. My legs don't tingle or crawl or feel uncomfortable like that. It's not a physical feeling in my legs specifically making me move them... it's just a general feeling of needing to be up and doing something. But no weird sensations in my legs.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what it feels like when you can't sit still? Is it more of an inner restlessness or a mental push to keep moving, even when you know you should be resting?\n\nPatient: It's definitely an inner restlessness. Honestly, that's really why I came in. I just feel like I cannot settle inside my own skin anymore. It's like... well, I just have to jump up and do the next thing, even when I'm completely worn out and I know I should just be sitting still. It's just a constant push to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Is this inner restlessness something you feel all the time, even when you're not in situations where you need to be still, or does it mostly come up when you're trying to sit down or relax?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's pretty much all the time. I mean... I guess I notice it the most when I actually try to sit down in my chair and relax, because that's when it really bothers me that I can't just be still. But even when I'm up and doing things around the house, I still feel that push. It's just always there, like my motor is always running, no matter what I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where your restlessness was so intense that it significantly interfered with your ability to function at work, socially, or in other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely interferes with things. It's gotten to the point where my family has actually had to step in and take over dealing with my bills and paperwork because I just can't sit down and focus on them. And socially... well, it's affected my volunteer work. The people at my church and the community garden have had to redo plans or cover for responsibilities I ended up dropping. I just couldn't stay on track with what I promised to do. It makes me feel really embarrassed, honestly, because these are groups I care about, and now they feel like they can't count on me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing faster than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... I always have that restless feeling, like I said, but it's not like I feel unusually energetic. It's actually pretty exhausting. And I definitely don't feel like I need less sleep. I mean, the bills and the volunteering are the main things it really messes up. I don't always realize I'm doing it, but then the ladies sitting beside me will start looking over at me. They don't always say something right away, but I can tell it's distracting them, and they've definitely pointed it out to me after.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been diagnosed with or experienced any issues with your sleep, such as insomnia or sleep apnea?\n\nPatient: No, I've never been diagnosed with any of that. No sleep apnea or anything. It's really just that trouble winding down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days or more where you felt unusually good, like you were on top of the world, or where you were much more talkative, active, or confident than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever experienced anything like that. It's like... well, I just have to jump up and do the next thing, even when I'm completely worn out and I know I should just be sitting still. It's just a constant push to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Is this inner restlessness something you feel all the time, even when you're not in situations where you need to be still, or does it mostly come up when you're trying to sit down or relax?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's pretty much all the time. But even when I'm up and doing things around the house, I still feel that push. My mood isn't elevated or unusually high. And I definitely need my sleep. Like I said, I'm completely worn out. It's just hard to actually sit still and get that rest, you know? But I certainly don't feel like I can just go without sleep, and I wouldn't say I'm irritable... mostly just tired of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad or anything like that. I still like my usual hobbies and such. It's just... well, like I said, the hard part is sitting still long enough to actually do them. But no, my mood is fine. I haven't been feeling down or losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling excessively worried or anxious about a number of things, like work, health, or other everyday matters?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have that problem. I mean, I have normal everyday concerns, but I'm not excessively worried or anxious about my health or anything else. My mind isn't spinning with worries... it's really just that physical feeling of needing to be doing something, like I can't just sit and relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a discomfort or restlessness in your legs when you try to sit still or lie down, like a crawling or tingling sensation that makes you want to move them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get anything like that. My legs don't tingle or crawl or feel uncomfortable like that. It's not a physical feeling in my legs specifically making me move them... it's just a general feeling of needing to be up and doing something. But no weird sensations in my legs.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what it feels like when you can't sit still? Is it more of an inner restlessness or a mental push to keep moving, even when you know you should be resting?\n\nPatient: It's definitely an inner restlessness. Honestly, that's really why I came in. I just feel like I cannot settle inside my own skin anymore. It's like... well, I just have to jump up and do the next thing, even when I'm completely worn out and I know I should just be sitting still. It's just a constant push to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Is this inner restlessness something you feel all the time, even when you're not in situations where you need to be still, or does it mostly come up when you're trying to sit down or relax?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's pretty much all the time. I mean... I guess I notice it the most when I actually try to sit down in my chair and relax, because that's when it really bothers me that I can't just be still. But even when I'm up and doing things around the house, I still feel that push. It's just always there, like my motor is always running, no matter what I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where your restlessness was so intense that it significantly interfered with your ability to function at work, socially, or in other important areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely interferes with things. It's gotten to the point where my family has actually had to step in and take over dealing with my bills and paperwork because I just can't sit down and focus on them. And socially... well, it's affected my volunteer work. The people at my church and the community garden have had to redo plans or cover for responsibilities I ended up dropping. I just couldn't stay on track with what I promised to do. It makes me feel really embarrassed, honestly, because these are groups I care about, and now they feel like they can't count on me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing faster than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... I always have that restless feeling, like I said, but it's not like I feel unusually energetic. It's actually pretty exhausting. And I definitely don't feel like I need less sleep. I don't always realize I'm doing it, but then the ladies sitting beside me will start looking over at me. They don't always say something right away, but I can tell it's distracting them, and they've definitely pointed it out to me after.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been diagnosed with or experienced any issues with your sleep, such as insomnia or sleep apnea?\n\nPatient: No, I've never been diagnosed with any of that. It's really just that trouble winding down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days or more where you felt unusually good, like you were on top of the world, or where you were much more talkative, active, or confident than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever experienced anything like that. It's just a constant push to keep going.\n\nDoctor: Is this inner restlessness something you feel all the time, even when you're not in situations where you need to be still, or does it mostly come up when you're trying to sit down or relax?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's pretty much all the time.     "additional_requirements": [
      "Symptoms not better explained by other mental disorder (e.g., mania, depression, anxiety, or restless legs syndrome)",
      "Absence of elevated or irritable mood, racing thoughts, or decreased need for sleep",
      "Absence of excessive worry typical of generalized anxiety disorder"
    ]
  }
}
